{"etymology_number":"1","lang_code":"en","pos":"noun","senses":[{"examples":[{"text":"The flower beds were white with grit from sand blasting the flagstone walkways.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["A collection of hard small materials, such as dirt, ground stone, debris from sandblasting or other such grinding, or swarf from metalworking."],"tags":["uncountable"]},{"glosses":["A collection of hard small materials, such as dirt, ground stone, debris from sandblasting or other such grinding, or swarf from metalworking.","Sand or a sand–salt mixture spread on wet and, especially, icy roads and footpaths to improve traction."],"tags":["uncountable"]},{"examples":[{"text":"These cookies seem to have grit from nutshells in them.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Small, hard, inedible particles in food."],"tags":["uncountable"]},{"examples":[{"text":"I need a sheet of 100 grit sandpaper.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["A measure of the size of abrasive grains, such as those on sandpaper, and thus their relative coarseness or fineness; the smaller the number, the coarser the abrasive: thus, 60 is rough, 600 is fine, and 3000 is ultrafine."],"tags":["uncountable"]},{"glosses":["A hard, coarse-grained siliceous sandstone; gritstone. Also, a finer sharp-grained sandstone, e.g., grindstone grit."],"tags":["uncountable"],"topics":["geography","geology","natural-sciences"]},{"examples":[{"text":"That kid with the cast on his arm has the grit to play dodgeball.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["Strength of mind; courage or fearlessness; fortitude."],"tags":["idiomatic","uncountable"]}],"sounds":[{"ipa":"/ɡɹɪt/"}],"upstream_line":259518,"upstream_offset":1292092295,"upstream_record_sha256":"07bc0c5d27f4d877a8dbd87dc4a9117964447b0fa4b020b15b5e03f9e12a3fa8","word":"grit"}
